A PROGRESSIVE INDUSTRY.

Messrs. Richmond's Annual Meeting.

THE NEW WORKS.

Another year of progress was under review at the sixteenth annual meeting of shareholders of the Rich- mond Gas Stove and Meter Company Ltd. which was held at the offices, Academy-street, Warrington, on Thursday. Mr. Thomas Dwerryhouse presided and amongst those present were Messrs. H. M. Thornton (vice-chairman), John Coxon, J. F. Atkins, 'A.B.. Bir- mingham, John A. Ransome and J. W. Glover, Chelsea (directors, Louis A. Voisey (auditor), J. \V. Slack (secretary), the Mayor (Alderman Smethurst), Messrs. W. Duffe, J. Booth, and Plant (shareholders).

===== 0002601_19070309_art0143.txt | included=true | included =====
THE REPORT.

The Directors reported that the balance standing to ' the credit of profit and loss account (after deducting debenture interest), including the amount brought for- ward as per balance sheet, was £31,768 17s. id., which the directors recommended be appropriated as follows: —Depreciation on buildings, patterns, plant, show- room fixtures, &c., £4,818 15s. ici. ; dividend on perfer- ewe capital at the rate of 6 per cent per annum, for the year ended December 31st, 1906, £5,933 oa. 10d. ; dividend (free of income tax) at the rate of 10 per cent per annum on the ordinary capital for the year ended December 31st, 1906, £8,036 12s. ; bonus (free of income tax) at the rate of 24 per cent per annum, £2,009 35.; Reserve fund (bringing up this fund to £40,000), £4,671; balance tla next account subject to directors' remunera- tion, £6,300 ss. Bcl. The business of the Company con- tinued to be satisfactory, and it was gratifying to the directors that they were able, after placing entire cost iof advertising as n former years against revenue, to again recommend a dividend at the rate of 10 per cent, and a bonus of 24 per cent in addition. The Company's works and various branches were in good condition and had been maintained out of revenue. During the year the sum of £15,432 19s. 6d. had been expended on the new Foundry at Grappenhall. The retiring Directors were Mr. J. A. Ransome and Mr. J. F. Atkins. Their re-election by the Board was reported in accordance with the Company's Articles of Association. The auditor, Mr. L. A. Voisey,chartered accountant, retired and was recommended for re-appointment. THE CHAIRMAN ON THE YEAR'S WORK. The CHAIRMAN said the shareholders were to be con- gratulated on having had a fairly prosperous year, although the profit made was less than that shown in the previous year. A decrease of about £l3OO was scarcely satisfactory but when they looked closely into the matter, they found good reasons why the profit should be less. In the first place competition was very keen in the business and when a number of concerns, some as large and others larger than their own, com- peted, the prices must drop and profits drop also. Again, though they had been very far-sighted in making con- tracts for materials largely used by them, the rise in all raw materials—tin, copper and the innumerable articles which they used—placed them at a great dis- advantage before they had a chance of revising their prices. These matters had partly adjusted themselves and they hoped to have better results in the present year. When he addressed them last year he mentioned the probability of their building a new foundry on the 94 acres of land they had purchased atGrappenhall. The new foundry was now an accomplished fact. It was com- menced eight months ago and was now accommodating about 200 workmen. (Applause). They had spent about £15,500 upon it. The foundry was a fine building cover- ing about 21 acreas and reflected credit upon the architects, builders, and those in the firm's employment who gave them the benefit of their ideas both in the building and in its furniture. The buildings were so planned that the foundry, which oocupied most space in comparison with the other parts of ths works, could be extended if necessary on two sides to almast double k its present extent. After giving particulars of the works, Mr. Dwerryhouse said he believed it was the finest foundry of the kind in this part of the country. (Applause). This was the first instalment of their centralised works and the directors hoped it would fulfil all their expectations. When the next part would be built they could not say. They would like it to be constructed very soon but circumstances might prevent their carrying it out. Turning to the accounts Mr. Dwerryhouse said the £15,000 expenditure on the new foundry had been taken from the cash they had in hand and transferred to the amount set forth in the assets for freehold and leasehold works, &c., which uow totalled over £BO,OOO. (Applause.) They had not to raise any capital or incur a loan to erect the foundry, as they took the money from cash in hand. The amount of trade stocks (234,182 us. 10d.) was a trifle above what it stood at last year but the sum due from debtors (£54,975 2s. 2d.) showed an increase of £4,000. They had, however, done a very much larger busintss, although the profit was smaller. Prices were lower than they ought to be and raw materials had gone up, so that they had to cover a great deal more ground, and consequently their debts were larger. The cash in hand was, of course, less, as the result of the expense incurred in the erec- tion of the foundry. Their investments remained as they were., and he was happy to say they had not sold

any to raise money for tlie -new building. They pro- posed to increase the reserve fund to £40,000, and he considered that was a very good feature. (Hear, hear.) They owed to creditors £13,000 as against £ll,OOO, but they must bear in mind that the debts due to them amounted to nearly £35,000, so that they were in a very sound position. The bad debts this year totalled £-42, and last year they were only £9. Having referred to other features of the balance sheet, Mr. Dwerryhouse said they were carry- ing forward to the next account the very respectable sum of £6,300. (Applause.) Although the profit was less, he considered the balance sheet was a very strong one and many friends to whom be had shown it had said they wished they were shareholders. He moved : That the directors' report and statement of accounts for the year ended December 31st, 1900, as certified by the auditor, be received and adopted. Mr. H. M. THORNTON seconded. He said that prob- ably to those who attended the meetings it became rather monotonous to see the regular way in which they paid dividends. (Laughter.) So long as monotony spelt profit, he was sure the shareholders would have no objection to it. In his opinion the financial strength of the balance sheet was by far the best they had ever produced. The reserve fund, which stood at £lO,OOO was shown in cash and liquid assets, that was to say it was invested in both forms outside their business—an important matter which the directors had

I always sought to achieve. They of the management %yore daily in touch with the working of the concern, and when he said they had had a most strenuous year he was sure the shareholders.would believe it was a fact. The fact of raw materials being up and prices down made it a most ditficalt year, but thanks to the splendid loyalty of the whole of the staff from top to bottom, they had achieved wonderful results. (Applause.) Not only were the thanks of the shareholders due to the staff but also to the dire3tors who, he could assure that meeting, were not there as figure heads but threw them- selves earnestly, into the direction of the affairs of the company. The Chairmati had expressed the hope that the present year would be better than the last, and they would all aim for that object. He could assure them that there was not one engaged in the management of the company who was not heart and soul in the work and when they had a staff animated by that spirit, he thought success was assured. Oa the other hand they muat have dillicult years to face and they had got one before them. They were not, however, going forward with any half-hearted feeling but with the intention of surmounting the diffi- culties and he hoped sincerely they would show equally satisfactory results at their next annual meeting. (Applause).

===== 0002601_19070309_art0144.txt | included=true | included =====
TEIE DIVIDEND.

The CIIA.IFtNIAN moved : Thit a dividend at the rate of 10 per cent. per annum (less 5 per cent. already paid) and a bonus of 21 per cent, for the year ended December 31st, 1906, free of income tax,be now declared and paid. Mr JOHN COXON seconded, and said the dividend had been thoroughly earned. He congratulated the direc- tors and shareholders upon the fact that the turnover had been greater during the past year than in any pre- vious year, and he hoped in future the price of manu- factured goods would be such as to repay them for the high prices they were now paying for raw materials. The resolution was carried. DIRECTORS' REMUNERATION.

Mr. Wruilm Dt7FFE moved :—" That the sum of £7OO be placed at the disposal of the directors for their services during the year 190 G." He believed that so long as the preseut directors, and particularly their worthy Chairman, remained on the Board, they might look forward to successful results. Mr. JOHN Booni seconded and the motion was agreed to.

===== 0002601_19070309_art0145.txt | included=true | included =====
THE AUDITOR.

The 'MAYOR proposed : "That Mr. L. A. Voisey be re-appointed auditor of the accounts of the company for the year 1907 at a remuneration of £150." He wished every auditor had the pleasure of delivering such an excellent balance sheet to the shareholders. Every year the position of the company improved. He hoped Mr. Voisey would always follow in the footsteps of his father, because they all knew him to be a straightforward man who would not put anything before the public that was not safe.

Mr. PLlN'r seconded and the resolution was carried. Mr. VOISEY returned thanks and recorded his appreci- ation of the great assistance he had received from the secretary, Mr. Slac'c.

Mr. DUFFN moved a vote of thanks to the Chairman. The MAyorc seconded and congratulated the company on the splendid way in which the new works had been constructed.

The vote was heartily accorded and the Chairman's reply concluded the meeting.

NOT ONCE LATE IN 40 YEARS.

John Hesketh, after forty years' service at Stretford Post Office, has retired on pension. It is estimated that during this long period he has covered not less than 180,000 miles and has never once been late.

14 YEARS' SUFFIgRING.

Anamia and Gastric Ulcers Cured by • VENO'S SEAWEED TONIC.

The story Mrs. EKcall, of The Glebe, Barming,, Maid- stone. lient, tells of the wonderful cure of her daughter by Veno's Seaweed Tonic, is of great interest. Mrs. Excell writes: —" ty daughter suffered terribly, for fourteen years, seven of which she was quite bed- ridden. Doctors attendad her for a long time and said she was suffering from gastric ulcers with consequent anrnia, but they could not do her any good. and at last gave up all hopes of her recovery and said she was incurable.

"The poor girl suffered tortures, and the pain of wind on the stomach was dreadful. However, seeming one day a little better, I carried her to a bath chair. She could not put her feet to the ground, and was helpless as a baby, but still she did not improve. "At last, seeing in the paper about your Sea Weed Tonic and what it had done for other sufferers, I deter- mined to try it. I got a small bottle, and after two or three doses it seemed to do her good, so I persevered and got her a small bottle every week. In three months time she could walk about as well as I can and she is now quite cured." For all skin and blood diseases, anaomia. weakness, and all digestive troubles, Veno's Seaweed Tonic is the safest and surest remedy known. It cures the worst cases of indigestion, torpid liver, flatulence, kidney trouble, and female weakness, and' a bottle should be in every home. Price is. 1.1. d. and 2s. 9d. of all chemists. For all-round purity and potency, Veno's Seaweed Tonic is unequalled.

WARRINGTON BOARD OF GUARDIANS. Proposed New Offices.

THE INCREASE OF PUBLIC EXPENDITURE.

A meeting of the Warrington Board of Guardians was held at the Workhouse on Wednesday, Mr. J. Billings presiding. There were also present :—Dr. Patterson, Mrs. Clay, Mrs. Wilkinson, Miss Broadbent, Messrs. W. Hickman, A. J. Jolley, R. Forshaw, S. Davies, J. Payton, R. Bolton, G. Thompson. A. Brooks, G. Mather, W. C. Brooks, J. Cook, W. Flannagan, T. Ritchie, M. W. Seton, E. L. Bent, J.. C. Sutton (clerk), J. F. Acvbery (master), and A. Wright (superintendent of Padgate schools.)

THE PROPOSED NEW OFFICES. THE BOARD'S EXPENDITURE. 1 Mr. THOMPSON pointed out that a Sites Committee had been appointed to consider the provision of new offices and on October 31st the membership was increased from five to twelve. He considered it was time the com- mittee presented a report on its work, as the matter was very important. He believed Messrs. Owen had been called in to advise, and as the Board would shortly go out of office he thought they were entitled to the information. He proposed that the Committee be requested to report at the next meeting. Continuing, Mr. Thompson said that most of the ratepayers and especially the overseers were looking upon the increase of rates with some alarm. As far as Great Sankey was concerned, the County Call for 1904-5 was £687. In 1905-6 it had advanced to £786, and for 1906-7 it was £867. That meant a rate of something like 41d. in the 2, and if the demand continued to increase like that he did not know how they would get along. He quite admitted the necessity of new offices, but the scheme ought to be carried out as economically as possible. Mr. ASHCROFT seconded, and said the matter had been kept in the dark a long time. Many ratepayers had asked him whether it was a fact that only two of the sites offered were left in for consideration, and that a Guardian was interested in one of them?

Mr. THOMPSON : I think Mr. Ashcroft is forestalling the question. We ought to allow the committee to report. The MIA IRMAN pointed out that the Board appointed the full committee as well as the architects. The com- mittee had already reported to the Board on several occasions and they were not in a position to present any further statement that morning. Mr. Ashcroft had hinted that things were being kept in the dark. They could not keep anything in the dark with reference to the question of buying land. It must come to the light. The question of purchasing a site would rest entirely with the new Board as it was necessary that twentyeight days'notice should be given to every Guardian before such a purchase was sanctioned. The gates Committee were not yet in a position to recommend a site. With regard to the County rate, the Board had no control of the county expenditure. He was delighted to think the Board bad approved a precept to meet all the expenses of the Union at a rate of Gd. in the 2.1 t was a credit to every member,and those who sought re-election could not have a better recommendation. (Laughter.) He hardly thought it necessary to put the resolution to the vote as he could say that in all probability the committee would report at an early date. Mr. TiromesoN pressed the resolution as he contended the Board should be in possession of all the facts. Mr. Jor,LEv. said it was important that they should have the truth in a plain straightforward way. Mr. Thompson had properly called attention to the increas- ing demands upon the overseers, but he (Mr. Jolley) did • not think members of the Board and, still more, the outside public who could not be supposed be ia posses- sion of the information which was available to every member of the Board, ought to be misled by a state- ment like that. The suggestion was made or implied that in some way or other the Guardians were respon- sible for the increasing rates. Mr. THONIPSON : No. Mr. JoLLF,v held that any outsider reading the report of Mr. Thompson's remarks would come to that conclusion.

Mr. TnomrsoN : I referred specially to the County rate.

Mr. JOLLEY : But how many people outside know how the County rate is made up? Most emphatically we as Guardians are entitled to say that it is not the Guardians who are responsible for the increased demand. In several important departments, continued Mr. Jolley, the expenditure of the Board had been reduced and .if only sound policy were adhered to the expenditure might be further reduced. For the last four years the expenditure at the Workhouse had been decreas- irg annually and the expenditure for the last year reckoning fuel, lighting, heating, keepirg the inmates and officers &c., was over .C7OO less than it was four years ago. That a Union like the Warrington Union could be administered for a call of less than Is. was not a thing to be ashamed of, but something to claim credit for.

11Tr. Bor,ToN said Mr. Jolley's statement startled him because he had been given to understand that the expenses at the House wereabont £1,200 in 1900 and over £2,000 last year, not reckoning the new laundry appliances.

•Nir. ASHCROFT : According to a report issued by the clerk about three months ago the expenditure was going up terribly. Mr. BOLTON asked for figures but the chairman ruled' that pa...ft of the discussion out of order.

_ Mr. HICKMAN objected to the Sites Committee being. call upon to give a full report. They ought, to keep. their business as secret as possible, especially with regard to the purchase of sites,as by publishing informa- tion they might stultify themselves. Mr. MATHER slid information had been given to the. committee confidentially. Mr. THOMPSON : All I want is a brief report as to where we stand in the matter. Mr. BENT objected to Mr. Ashcroft's remarks to the effect that the committee were keeping something in the dark. Nothing definite would be done without the knowledge of the Board. Mr. THOMPSON explained that he did not make his statement with regard to the county expenditure with any intention to mislead. He had never made a state- ment in his life with the intention of misleading the public. He said distinctly that he was referring to the County rate. The resolution was carried, only five membeis,includ- ing Mr. Jolley, voting against it. Mr. BOLTON said he thought Mr. Jolley had no right to vote as he was interested. Mr. BENT: I object to that remark. This concluded the meeting.

Handball Warrington Gymnasium Handball Team journeyed to Egremont on Wednesday night, to fulfil their league engagement with Egremont St. Johns. The game was played at a quick and rough pace, and many exciting scrimmages took place in which Skinner, of the Egremont Tqam was always prominent. How- ever, despite the rough tackling which was allowed to continue, and the disadvantage to the Warrington Team of the Gymnasium being smaller than their own Topping, for the visitors secured the first goal after a round of passing. Egremont depended more on long shots and after some several good throws, equalized just shots, all half-time. The play through the second ' half continued at a rough pace, and owing to the keen tackling of the backs, on both sides, the forwards were not allowed to steady themselves for throwing, and although each side had a penalty thr.,w no further goals were registered. Final result : Warrington 1 goal, Egremont 1 goal. A FROZEN J.P. Alderman J. Massey, J.P. wrote to the St. Helens Town Council on Wednesday complaining of the dis- comforts of the magisterial bench. In his letter he said: 1" I was fairly frozen for two or three hours and when I got away from the business I had to give way, and have been laid up at home ever since. The want of comfort on the bench makes me say I will not sit again until the heating and ventilating is put right." Mr. Sidney Buxton has reinstated the young Post j Office servant, C4orst, who was dismissed from Stock— port Post Office for no better reason than that he lived under the roof of his father, a hotelkeeper at Tiviot I Dale.

GOLF. WARRINGTON CLUB. The final for the Captain's prize was played at the links at Appleton on Saturday, and resulted in a win for Mr. E. L. Robinson with a gross score of 95, less handi- cap 16, net score 79. The green and courses were in excellent condition considering the time of the year, and the fact that they had been closed since October. The best gross score of the day was returned by Dr. Smith, with the gross score of 89, handicap six, net 83, This card wins the sweep. Runcorn Union. STOCKTON HEATH. As was expected a contest is announced in Stockton Heath, Messrs. Hallows and Whitley being opposed by Mr. Mynekyme and Councillor Pemberton. Councillor Pemberton, Fairfield-road, builder, is pro- posed by Mr. G. J. Brown and seconded by Councillor Lord. * John Hallows, Grappenhall-road, gentleman, is proposed by John McDerruid, seconded by J. W. Peters. * James Whitley, Chapel-lane, grocer, is proposed by C. W. Davenport, seconded by J. A. Howard. John Mynekyme, London-road, gentleman is proposed by E. Caldwell, seconded by C. W. Davenport. For Latchford Without Mr. G. Pilling has been re-elected unopposed, whilst Mr. W. Weir retains the representation of Walton. COUNTY CONSTITUENCIES. MR. J. W. KNOWLES DEFEATED. For the first time in the history of the Winwick division a contest has taken place for the appointment of a representative on the Lancashire County Council. For nine years Mr. J. W. Knowles, Houghton. solicitor, had been returned unopposed but this year Mr. Richard Forshaw, Burtonwood, brewer, has successfully opposed his return. Both gentlenaen entered with spirit into the contest, which whilst conducted with vigour was fortunately free from personalities. Meetings were held and the division was energetically canvassed by both candidates. The election took place on Thursday but, polling was only light, and resulted as follows : Mr. Forshaw Mr. Knowles Majority The result was declared at, Padgate shortly after.nine o'clock and was received with evident satisfaction by a fairly large crowd which had assembled. Immediately after the counting the usual vote of thanks was accorded to the returning officer (Mr. E. J. Sutton) and the presiding officers &c., on the motion of Mr. Forshaw seconded by Mr. Knowles. Mr. Forshaw during a brief speech spoke in the very high terms of the assistance which the officials had rendered to him. He was sure that he had given them a great deal of trouble but he had always been received with the greatest courtesy. In the Dareshury Division of the Cheshire County Council %Tr. A. G. Guest, Weston, and Mr. James Mason Frith were the candidates for a seat rendered vacant by the retirement of Mr. Occleson. The voting resulted as follows : Mr. Guest Mr. Frith 340 Majority In the Frodsham Divisiceit of the Cheshire County Council Mr. T. Baxter, Stonton Heath, defeated the retiring member, Mr. Francis Boston, Runcorn, the voting being : Mr. Baxter Mr. Boston i‘lajority
